1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""lr-core — deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps of lore skills.
3	
4	Part of the lore-framework plugin (`lr`). Pure Python 3 standard library, no
5	pip install, one file — the same sanctioned-exception class as `scripts/lrb.py`
6	and `scripts/wait-server.py` (see docs/conventions.md § Tooling: Non-Shell
7	Runtimes).
8	
9	WHAT THIS IS. Boot, attach, consult, recall and finalize each carry a large
10	mechanical half — scan the workspace, pull a repo, compare a version stamp,
11	detect a teammate spawn, enumerate lore topics — that was historically executed
12	as prose by the model, one tool call per step. That work needs no judgment, so
13	it belongs in code: it runs in one round-trip instead of six, it can't drift
14	between engines, and it encodes the platform traps (macOS `ps` field packing,
15	missing GNU `timeout`, HTTPS credential prompts) once instead of in every doc.
16	
17	WHAT THIS IS NOT. This is not a fast path over a separate prose specification.
18	The step comments inside each cmd_* function below (and the helpers they call —
19	pull_repo, compare_versions, detect_teammate, _resolve_agent) ARE the normative
20	procedure: read them in order and you have the full hand-executable spec, not
21	a summary of one. docs/*.md files carry short pointers into specific functions
22	here, not restated procedures — a restated copy is exactly the drift risk this
23	avoids. Anything requiring judgment still stays with the model: applying
24	migrations, deciding what lore means, composing summaries, resolving
25	conflicts. See docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract (the "literate
26	accelerator" pattern) — when this script cannot complete, the caller notifies
27	the user, reads the relevant function's comments below, and executes those
28	steps by hand.
29	
30	SUBCOMMANDS
31	  discover    Enumerate lore agent repos and their agents in a workspace.
32	  preflight   Boot/attach/consult preflight for one agent: resolve, pull
33	              (TTL-cached), compare versions, detect teammate spawn.
34	  scan        Manifest of an agent's lore topics with git dates and staleness.
35	
36	OUTPUT CONTRACT. Every subcommand prints exactly one JSON object:
37	
38	    {"ok": bool, "data": {...}, "warnings": [str], "errors": [str]}
39	
40	  `ok`   — was the request satisfied? A determinate negative answer (no such
41	           agent) is ok:false with exit 0: the script functioned, the caller
42	           acts on `errors`/`data` per its procedure doc. No takeover needed.
43	
44	EXIT CODES
45	  0  The script ran to completion; `data` is authoritative. Degraded conditions
46	     (pull failed, `ps` blocked) are reported *inside* data — they are results,
47	     not failures, exactly as the prose procedures treat them.
48	  2  The script could not complete. The caller must fall back to the prose
49	     procedure per the Script Fallback Contract. Callers treat any unexpected
50	     exit code, absent output, or unparsable output as 2 as well.
51	
52	Floor: Python 3.9. No `match`, no `X | Y` annotations.
53	"""
54	
55	import argparse
56	import json
57	import os
58	import re
59	import subprocess
60	import sys
61	import time
62	
63	# Bound every git subprocess. The Bash-tool timeout that bounds prose-driven
64	# pulls does not exist here, so the script owns its own backstop.
65	GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC = 60
66	PS_TIMEOUT_SEC = 10
67	# `run()` returns this when a command never executed — missing binary, broken
68	# PATH, or a timeout. Distinct from any real exit status of the command itself.
69	GIT_UNRUNNABLE = -1
70	DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC = 600
71	DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS = 180
72	# How far up the process tree to look for the Agent-Teams marker. The engine is
73	# typically the grandparent (script -> shell -> engine); the extra headroom
74	# covers wrapper scripts.
75	ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH = 6
76	TEAMMATE_MARKER = "--agent-id"
77	# Flag-boundary match, not a substring test: `--agent-idle-timeout=30` in some
78	# unrelated ancestor must not read as the Agent-Teams marker.
79	TEAMMATE_MARKER_RE = re.compile(r"(?:^|\s)--agent-id(?:[\s=]|$)")
80	TITLE_MAX_CHARS = 120
81	
82	
83	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
84	# Result plumbing
85	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
86	
87	class Result(object):
88	    """Accumulates the single JSON object a subcommand prints."""
89	
90	    def __init__(self):
91	        self.ok = True
92	        self.data = {}
93	        self.warnings = []
94	        self.errors = []
95	
96	    def warn(self, msg):
97	        self.warnings.append(msg)
98	
99	    def fail(self, msg):
100	        """A determinate negative answer. Still exit 0 — the script functioned."""
101	        self.ok = False
102	        self.errors.append(msg)
103	
104	    def emit(self):
105	        payload = {
106	            "ok": self.ok,
107	            "data": self.data,
108	            "warnings": self.warnings,
109	            "errors": self.errors,
110	        }
111	        sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=False) + "\n")
112	        return 0
113	
114	
115	def emit_fatal(msg):
116	    """The script could not complete: exit 2, caller falls back to prose."""
117	    payload = {"ok": False, "data": {}, "warnings": [], "errors": [msg]}
118	    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
119	    return 2
120	
121	
122	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
123	# Small helpers
124	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
125	
126	def read_text(path):
127	    try:
128	        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
129	            return fh.read()
130	    except (IOError, OSError):
131	        return None
132	
133	
134	def run(argv, timeout, env_extra=None, cwd=None):
135	    """Run a command. Returns (rc, stdout, stderr); rc -1 on timeout/missing binary.
136	
137	    Never raises — a missing binary or a hung remote is data for the caller,
138	    not an exception that aborts a boot.
139	    """
140	    env = os.environ.copy()
141	    if env_extra:
142	        env.update(env_extra)
143	    try:
144	        proc = subprocess.run(
145	            argv,
146	            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
147	            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
148	            timeout=timeout,
149	            env=env,
150	            cwd=cwd,
151	        )
152	        return (
153	            proc.returncode,
154	            proc.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
155	            proc.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
156	        )
157	    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
158	        return (-1, "", "timed out after %ss" % timeout)
159	    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
160	        return (-1, "", str(exc))
161	
162	
163	def _git_unrunnable(err):
164	    """The uniform result for a git call that never executed.
165	
166	    Applies at EVERY git call site, not just the first one. `git` failing to run
167	    (absent binary, broken PATH, timeout) is not the same answer as git running
168	    and saying no — collapsing the two lets a broken toolchain masquerade as a
169	    legitimate "not a repo" / "no origin remote" skip, under an exit code that
170	    promises the data is authoritative.
171	    """
172	    return {"status": "failed",
173	            "detail": "could not run git: %s" % (err.strip()[:200] or "unknown")}
174	
175	
176	def git(repo, args, timeout=GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC, env_extra=None):
177	    """Run git against `repo` with -C. Never `cd` — the shell CWD is shared
178	    with the caller's other tools (docs/conventions.md § Tooling: CWD Safety).
179	
180	    Every invocation is forced to the C locale: some outcomes are detected by
181	    reading git's human-readable stdout, which is translated under an NLS-
182	    enabled git in a non-English locale.
183	    """
184	    env = {"LC_ALL": "C", "LANGUAGE": "C"}
185	    if env_extra:
186	        env.update(env_extra)
187	    return run(["git", "-C", repo] + args, timeout, env_extra=env)
188	
189	
190	def parse_frontmatter(text):
191	    """Parse the minimal YAML subset the framework uses in descriptor files.
192	
193	    Supports `key: value` (optionally quoted) and block sequences of the form
194	    `key:` followed by `  - item` lines. Not a general YAML parser and does not
195	    need to be — descriptor schemas are fixed by docs/conventions.md.
196	    """
197	    out = {}
198	    if not text:
199	        return out
200	    lines = text.split("\n")
201	    if not lines or lines[0].strip() != "---":
202	        return out
203	    body = []
204	    for line in lines[1:]:
205	        if line.strip() == "---":
206	            break
207	        body.append(line)
208	    current_list_key = None
209	    for line in body:
210	        if not line.strip() or line.lstrip().startswith("#"):
211	            continue
212	        item = re.match(r"^\s+-\s+(.*)$", line)
213	        if item and current_list_key:
214	            out[current_list_key].append(_unquote(item.group(1).strip()))
215	            continue
216	        kv = re.match(r"^([A-Za-z0-9_-]+):\s*(.*)$", line)
217	        if not kv:
218	            continue
219	        key, val = kv.group(1), kv.group(2).strip()
220	        if val == "":
221	            out[key] = []
222	            current_list_key = key
223	        else:
224	            out[key] = _unquote(val)
225	            current_list_key = None
226	    return out
227	
228	
229	def _unquote(val):
230	    if len(val) >= 2 and val[0] == val[-1] and val[0] in ("'", '"'):
231	        return val[1:-1]
232	    return val
233	
234	
235	def resolve_framework_root(explicit):
236	    """Framework root = the directory containing VERSION.
237	
238	    Defaults to this script's parent directory (scripts/ lives at the root).
239	    Boot Step 0 resolves the root for the *engine*; this is the script's own
240	    independent resolution so it can be run standalone.
241	    """
242	    if explicit:
243	        return os.path.abspath(explicit)
244	    return os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.realpath(__file__)))
245	
246	
247	def framework_version(root):
248	    text = read_text(os.path.join(root, "VERSION"))
249	    return text.strip() if text else None
250	
251	
252	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
253	# Discovery
254	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
255	
256	def find_repos(workspace):
257	    """Top-level dirs of `workspace` containing lore-repo.md.
258	
259	    Top-level only, deliberately: nested repos are invisible to the framework
260	    (docs/conventions.md § Directory Structure). Hidden dirs are skipped, which
261	    also excludes the .worktrees/ convention.
262	    """
263	    repos = []
264	    try:
265	        entries = sorted(os.listdir(workspace))
266	    except (IOError, OSError):
267	        return repos
268	    for name in entries:
269	        if name.startswith("."):
270	            continue
271	        path = os.path.join(workspace, name)
272	        if not os.path.isdir(path):
273	            continue
274	        descriptor = os.path.join(path, "lore-repo.md")
275	        if os.path.isfile(descriptor):
276	            repos.append(path)
277	    return repos
278	
279	
280	def describe_repo(repo_path):
281	    fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(os.path.join(repo_path, "lore-repo.md")))
282	    repos_field = fm.get("repos", [])
283	    if isinstance(repos_field, str):
284	        repos_field = [repos_field] if repos_field else []
285	    return {
286	        "path": repo_path,
287	        "name": os.path.basename(repo_path),
288	        "description": fm.get("description"),
289	        "version": fm.get("version"),
290	        "repos": repos_field,
291	    }
292	
293	
294	def repo_agents(repo_path):
295	    """Agents in one repo: agents/<name>/ containing role.md."""
296	    agents = []
297	    agents_dir = os.path.join(repo_path, "agents")
298	    if not os.path.isdir(agents_dir):
299	        return agents
300	    try:
301	        names = sorted(os.listdir(agents_dir))
302	    except (IOError, OSError):
303	        return agents
304	    for name in names:
305	        if name.startswith("."):
306	            continue
307	        agent_dir = os.path.join(agents_dir, name)
308	        role = os.path.join(agent_dir, "role.md")
309	        if not os.path.isfile(role):
310	            continue
311	        fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(role))
312	        agents.append({
313	            "name": name,
314	            "dir": agent_dir,
315	            "repo": repo_path,
316	            "description": fm.get("description"),
317	            "has_lore_context": os.path.isfile(
318	                os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore-context.md")),
319	        })
320	    return agents
321	
322	
323	def discover_workspace(workspace):
324	    repos, agents = [], []
325	    for repo_path in find_repos(workspace):
326	        info = describe_repo(repo_path)
327	        found = repo_agents(repo_path)
328	        info["agents"] = [a["name"] for a in found]
329	        repos.append(info)
330	        agents.extend(found)
331	    return repos, agents
332	
333	
334	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
335	# Git: TTL-cached auto-pull
336	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
337	
338	def absolute_git_dir(repo):
339	    rc, out, _ = git(repo, ["rev-parse", "--absolute-git-dir"], timeout=15)
340	    if rc == 0 and out.strip():
341	        return out.strip()
342	    return None
343	
344	
345	def _stamp_path(repo):
346	    gd = absolute_git_dir(repo)
347	    if not gd:
348	        return None
349	    # Inside the git dir: never committed, no .gitignore change needed, and it
350	    # follows the checkout when the worktree convention moves it.
351	    return os.path.join(gd, "lr-last-pull")
352	
353	
354	def _read_stamp(repo):
355	    path = _stamp_path(repo)
356	    if not path:
357	        return None
358	    text = read_text(path)
359	    if not text:
360	        return None
361	    try:
362	        return float(text.strip())
363	    except ValueError:
364	        return None
365	
366	
367	def _write_stamp(repo):
368	    path = _stamp_path(repo)
369	    if not path:
370	        return
371	    try:
372	        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
373	            fh.write("%d\n" % int(time.time()))
374	    except (IOError, OSError):
375	        pass  # A stamp we cannot write only costs us the TTL optimization.
376	
377	
378	def pull_repo(repo, ttl=DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC, fresh=False, do_pull=True):
379	    """Manual fallback: the exact TTL-cached auto-pull procedure for one repo.
380	
381	    Step 1: if `do_pull` is False (--no-pull), do nothing — report "disabled".
382	
383	    Step 2: `git -C <repo> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`. A git that cannot
384	    run at all (missing binary, broken PATH, timeout) is a "failed" result,
385	    never a "skipped" one — see _git_unrunnable above for why that
386	    distinction matters. If the command runs but says false or exits nonzero:
387	    not a git repo (or a bare repo with no work tree) — "skipped", not a
388	    failure, and stop here.
389	
390	    Step 3: `git -C <repo> remote get-url origin`. No origin remote — again
391	    "skipped", not a failure. Stop here.
392	
393	    Step 4 (TTL check): unless `fresh` or `ttl<=0`, read the timestamp at
394	    `<absolute-git-dir>/lr-last-pull` (never committed, resolved via
395	    `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` so it follows a worktree checkout). If
396	    it exists and is younger than `ttl` seconds, report "fresh" and stop —
397	    skip the network round-trip entirely.
398	
399	    Step 5 (the pull itself): run `git -C <repo> pull --ff-only` with
400	    `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o
401	    ConnectTimeout=10'` so an unauthenticated HTTPS/SSH remote fails fast
402	    instead of hanging on a prompt. (Do not wrap this in a `timeout`/
403	    `gtimeout` binary — GNU coreutils, absent on macOS/BSD; the caller's own
404	    subprocess timeout is the backstop.) Non-zero exit — "failed", with the
405	    last line of stderr/stdout as detail; the surrounding flow continues in
406	    degraded mode, this is not fatal.
407	
408	    Step 6: on success, write the current time into the TTL stamp file (a
409	    write failure here only costs the TTL optimization, not correctness).
410	    Report "up-to-date" if the output says so, else "pulled" — try to count
411	    commits via `git rev-list HEAD@{1}..HEAD --count` and include the count
412	    when it parses.
413	
414	    Returns a dict; never raises. Statuses:
415	      disabled     — caller passed --no-pull
416	      fresh        — skipped, pulled within the TTL window
417	      up-to-date   — pull ran, nothing to fetch
418	      pulled       — fast-forwarded (commits counted when derivable)
419	      skipped      — not a git repo, or no origin remote (legitimate states)
420	      failed       — network/auth/non-fast-forward, or git itself could not
421	                     run at all; caller continues degraded
422	    """
423	    if not do_pull:
424	        return {"status": "disabled", "detail": "pull suppressed by --no-pull"}
425	
426	    rc, out, err = git(repo, ["rev-parse", "--is-inside-work-tree"], timeout=15)
427	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
428	        return _git_unrunnable(err)
429	    if rc != 0:
430	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "not a git repo"}
431	    if out.strip() == "false":
432	        # Exits 0 inside a bare repo's own directory, but there is no work tree.
433	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "bare repository (no work tree)"}
434	
435	    rc, _, err = git(repo, ["remote", "get-url", "origin"], timeout=15)
436	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
437	        return _git_unrunnable(err)
438	    if rc != 0:
439	        return {"status": "skipped", "detail": "no origin remote"}
440	
441	    if not fresh and ttl > 0:
442	        stamp = _read_stamp(repo)
443	        if stamp is not None:
444	            age = time.time() - stamp
445	            if 0 <= age < ttl:
446	                return {
447	                    "status": "fresh",
448	                    "detail": "pulled %ds ago (ttl %ds)" % (int(age), ttl),
449	                    "age_seconds": int(age),
450	                }
451	
452	    # Per-transport fast-fail niceties over the subprocess-timeout backstop.
453	    # GNU `timeout` is deliberately not used — it is absent on macOS/BSD
454	    # (docs/conventions.md § Tooling: Portable Shell).
455	    env = {
456	        "GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT": "0",
457	        "GIT_SSH_COMMAND": "ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10",
458	    }
459	    rc, out, err = git(repo, ["pull", "--ff-only"], env_extra=env)
460	    if rc != 0:
461	        detail = (err or out or "unknown error").strip().split("\n")
462	        return {"status": "failed", "detail": detail[-1][:400]}
463	
464	    _write_stamp(repo)
465	
466	    if "Already up to date" in out or "Already up-to-date" in out:
467	        return {"status": "up-to-date", "detail": "already up to date"}
468	
469	    commits = None
470	    crc, cout, _ = git(repo, ["rev-list", "HEAD@{1}..HEAD", "--count"], timeout=15)
471	    if crc == 0 and cout.strip().isdigit():
472	        commits = int(cout.strip())
473	    result = {"status": "pulled", "detail": "fast-forwarded"}
474	    if commits:
475	        result["commits"] = commits
476	        result["detail"] = "fast-forwarded %d commit(s)" % commits
477	    return result
478	
479	
480	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
481	# Version comparison (detection only — migrations stay with the model)
482	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
483	
484	def compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version):
485	    """Manual fallback: how to read repo_version against fw_version by hand.
486	
487	    Either missing/unreadable -> "unknown". Equal as strings -> "match". Else
488	    try both as integers: lower repo version -> "repo-behind", higher ->
489	    "repo-ahead". If either isn't a plain integer -> "differs" ("compare
490	    manually" — some agent repos may use a non-numeric scheme).
491	
492	    This function only detects skew; it never decides what to do about it.
493	    Applying migrations is judgment work: docs/version-check.md.
494	    """
495	    out = {"repo": repo_version, "framework": fw_version}
496	    if repo_version is None or fw_version is None:
497	        out["verdict"] = "unknown"
498	        out["detail"] = "missing or unreadable version stamp"
499	        return out
500	    if str(repo_version).strip() == str(fw_version).strip():
501	        out["verdict"] = "match"
502	        return out
503	    try:
504	        rv, fv = int(str(repo_version).strip()), int(str(fw_version).strip())
505	    except ValueError:
506	        out["verdict"] = "differs"
507	        out["detail"] = "non-numeric version stamp(s); compare manually"
508	        return out
509	    out["verdict"] = "repo-behind" if rv < fv else "repo-ahead"
510	    return out
511	
512	
513	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
514	# Teammate detection
515	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
516	
517	def _ps_field(pid, field):
518	    """Read one ps field for one pid.
519	
520	    One field per call, deliberately: `ps -o ppid=,args=` packs multiple fields
521	    onto a single line, which has already produced a real parsing bug in this
522	    codebase (lore: macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md). Trailing `=`
523	    suppresses the header.
524	    """
525	    rc, out, _ = run(["ps", "-o", field + "=", "-p", str(pid)], PS_TIMEOUT_SEC)
526	    if rc != 0:
527	        return None
528	    val = out.strip()
529	    return val if val else None
530	
531	
532	def detect_teammate():
533	    """Manual fallback: walk process ancestry for the Agent-Teams marker.
534	
535	    Step 1: start at `os.getppid()` (this process's immediate parent).
536	
537	    Step 2: for each ancestor pid, up to ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH (6) hops or until
538	    pid 1: run `ps -o args= -p <pid>` (one field per call — packing multiple
539	    `-o` fields onto one line has already caused a real parsing bug here, see
540	    lore macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md). If `ps` returns nothing
541	    (blocked/unavailable), stop climbing and fall through to "unknown" below.
542	
543	    Step 3: test that process's args against `--agent-id` using a
544	    flag-boundary regex, not a substring search — `--agent-idle-timeout=30`
545	    in some unrelated ancestor must not false-positive. A match is an
546	    immediate "yes": report only that one matching pid/args (truncated to 200
547	    chars), not the full ancestry — the point of doing this in code instead
548	    of prose is exactly to avoid spending tokens on the whole chain.
549	
550	    Step 4: no match — fetch that process's ppid via another single-field
551	    `ps` call and climb to it; stop if the parent is unreadable/non-numeric or
552	    would loop back to the same pid.
553	
554	    Step 5: if the loop climbed zero ancestors (the very first `ps` call
555	    failed) -> "unknown" ("ps unavailable or blocked; assume host session").
556	    If it climbed at least one without a match -> "no".
557	
558	    Returns verdict yes/no/unknown. `unknown` is a first-class answer:
559	    sandboxed engines block `ps`, and the profiles' capability gates expect
560	    that.
561	    """
562	    pid = os.getppid()
563	    depth = 0
564	    scanned = 0
565	    while pid and pid > 1 and depth < ANCESTOR_SCAN_DEPTH:
566	        args = _ps_field(pid, "args")
567	        if args is None:
568	            break
569	        scanned += 1
570	        if TEAMMATE_MARKER_RE.search(args):
571	            # Only the matching process is echoed back. The full ancestry is
572	            # noise the caller pays input tokens for; brevity is the point of
573	            # moving this off the prose path in the first place.
574	            return {
575	                "verdict": "yes",
576	                "detail": "found %s in ancestor pid %d" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, pid),
577	                "matched": {"pid": pid, "args": args[:200]},
578	                "scanned": scanned,
579	            }
580	        parent = _ps_field(pid, "ppid")
581	        if not parent or not parent.strip().isdigit():
582	            break
583	        nxt = int(parent.strip())
584	        if nxt == pid:
585	            break
586	        pid = nxt
587	        depth += 1
588	    if not scanned:
589	        return {
590	            "verdict": "unknown",
591	            "detail": "ps unavailable or blocked; assume host session",
592	            "scanned": 0,
593	        }
594	    return {
595	        "verdict": "no",
596	        "detail": "no %s in %d ancestor(s)" % (TEAMMATE_MARKER, scanned),
597	        "scanned": scanned,
598	    }
599	
600	
601	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
602	# Lore topic scan
603	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
604	
605	def topic_title(path):
606	    text = read_text(path)
607	    if not text:
608	        return None
609	    for line in text.split("\n"):
610	        stripped = line.strip()
611	        if not stripped:
612	            continue
613	        stripped = re.sub(r"^#+\s*", "", stripped)
614	        stripped = re.sub(r"^[*_]+|[*_]+$", "", stripped).strip()
615	        if stripped:
616	            return stripped[:TITLE_MAX_CHARS]
617	    return None
618	
619	
620	def git_last_modified(repo, rel_dir):
621	    """Map repo-relative topic path -> last commit ISO date, in ONE git call.
622	
623	    Per-file `git log` would be one subprocess per topic (~175 today). The log
624	    walks newest-first, so the first date seen for a path is its last change.
625	
626	    Returns (dates, error). `error` is non-None only when git could not be run —
627	    the caller must not report committed topics as "undated" in that case, which
628	    would explain a broken toolchain as missing commits.
629	    """
630	    dates = {}
631	    rc, out, err = git(
632	        repo,
633	        ["log", "--format=%x1e%cI", "--name-only", "--", rel_dir],
634	        timeout=GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC,
635	    )
636	    if rc == GIT_UNRUNNABLE:
637	        return dates, "could not run git: %s" % (err.strip()[:200] or "unknown")
638	    if rc != 0:
639	        return dates, None
640	    current = None
641	    for line in out.split("\n"):
642	        if line.startswith("\x1e"):
643	            current = line[1:].strip()
644	            continue
645	        name = line.strip()
646	        if name and current and name not in dates:
647	            dates[name] = current
648	    return dates, None
649	
650	
651	def _age_days(iso_date):
652	    if not iso_date:
653	        return None
654	    try:
655	        # %cI is strict ISO-8601 with a numeric offset (e.g. +02:00). Python
656	        # 3.9's fromisoformat does not accept the colon in the offset on all
657	        # builds, so normalize before parsing.
658	        import datetime
659	        normalized = re.sub(r"([+-]\d{2}):(\d{2})$", r"\1\2", iso_date)
660	        parsed = datetime.datetime.strptime(normalized, "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z")
661	        delta = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc) - parsed
662	        return max(0, int(delta.total_seconds() // 86400))
663	    except (ValueError, ImportError):
664	        return None
665	
666	
667	def scan_lore(agent_dir, stale_days=DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS):
668	    lore_dir = os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore")
669	    if not os.path.isdir(lore_dir):
670	        return None, "no lore/ directory at %s" % lore_dir
671	
672	    try:
673	        names = sorted(
674	            n for n in os.listdir(lore_dir)
675	            if n.endswith(".md") and not n.startswith(".")
676	            # A directory named `something.md` is not a topic. Without this the
677	            # entry survives as a title-less, plausible-looking row.
678	            and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(lore_dir, n)))
679	    except (IOError, OSError) as exc:
680	        return None, str(exc)
681	
682	    repo = _repo_root_for(agent_dir)
683	    dates, git_error = {}, None
684	    if repo:
685	        rel_dir = os.path.relpath(lore_dir, repo)
686	        dates, git_error = git_last_modified(repo, rel_dir)
687	
688	    topics, undated, stale_count = [], 0, 0
689	    for name in names:
690	        path = os.path.join(lore_dir, name)
691	        rel = os.path.relpath(path, repo) if repo else name
692	        iso = dates.get(rel)
693	        age = _age_days(iso)
694	        if iso is None:
695	            undated += 1
696	        is_stale = age is not None and age > stale_days
697	        if is_stale:
698	            stale_count += 1
699	        try:
700	            size = os.path.getsize(path)
701	        except (IOError, OSError):
702	            size = None
703	        topics.append({
704	            "file": name,
705	            "title": topic_title(path),
706	            "last_modified": iso,
707	            "age_days": age,
708	            "stale": is_stale,
709	            "bytes": size,
710	        })
711	
712	    return {
713	        "lore_dir": lore_dir,
714	        "git_error": git_error,
715	        "count": len(topics),
716	        "stale_days_threshold": stale_days,
717	        "stale_count": stale_count,
718	        "undated_count": undated,
719	        "topics": topics,
720	    }, None
721	
722	
723	def _repo_root_for(agent_dir):
724	    """agents/<name>/ -> repo root, verified by lore-repo.md."""
725	    candidate = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(agent_dir)))
726	    if os.path.isfile(os.path.join(candidate, "lore-repo.md")):
727	        return candidate
728	    return None
729	
730	
731	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
732	# Subcommands
733	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
734	
735	def cmd_discover(args, res):
736	    """Manual fallback (§ Script Fallback Contract): enumerate agents by hand.
737	
738	    Step 1: resolve <workspace> to an absolute path. If it isn't a directory,
739	    stop — report "workspace not found" and return empty repos/agents.
740	
741	    Step 2: list top-level entries of <workspace> (skip dotfiles/dirs — this
742	    also skips the .worktrees/ convention) and keep the ones that are
743	    directories containing a lore-repo.md file. Each one is a lore agent repo
744	    (see find_repos above for the exact listing logic).
745	
746	    Step 3: for each repo found, read lore-repo.md's frontmatter for
747	    description/version/repos (see describe_repo), then list agents/*/ and
748	    keep the subdirectories that contain a role.md (see repo_agents) — read
749	    each role.md's frontmatter for its description.
750	
751	    Step 4: report the combined repos + agents list. If zero repos were found,
752	    that is a failure (ok:false), not silence — the caller needs to know no
753	    lore agent repos exist at this workspace root.
754	    """
755	    workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
756	    if not os.path.isdir(workspace):
757	        res.fail("workspace not found: %s" % workspace)
758	        res.data = {"workspace": workspace, "repos": [], "agents": []}
759	        return res
760	
761	    repos, agents = discover_workspace(workspace)
762	    res.data = {
763	        "workspace": workspace,
764	        "repos": repos,
765	        "agents": [{"name": a["name"], "repo": a["repo"], "dir": a["dir"],
766	                    "description": a["description"]} for a in agents],
767	    }
768	    if not repos:
769	        res.fail("no lore agent repos found in %s "
770	                 "(no top-level directory contains lore-repo.md)" % workspace)
771	    return res
772	
773	
774	def _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace):
775	    """Manual fallback: locate the target agent by hand.
776	
777	    Step 1: if --agent-dir was given, use it directly. Require a role.md
778	    inside it (else fail: "not an agent directory"). Derive the repo root by
779	    walking up two directories and checking for lore-repo.md there; if that
780	    check fails, warn (don't fail) that repo-scoped steps (pull, version) will
781	    be skipped for this agent. Read role.md's frontmatter for description.
782	
783	    Step 2: otherwise, discover every repo under <workspace> (see
784	    discover_workspace above) and filter its agents to the one(s) named
785	    --agent. Zero matches -> fail "agent not found", and report the full
786	    available_agents list so the caller can show it to the user. More than
787	    one match (same agent name in two repos) -> warn about the ambiguity and
788	    use the first one, arbitrarily.
789	
790	    Returns an agent dict, or None with res populated.
791	    """
792	    if args.agent_dir:
793	        agent_dir = os.path.abspath(args.agent_dir)
794	        role = os.path.join(agent_dir, "role.md")
795	        if not os.path.isfile(role):
796	            res.fail("no role.md at %s — not an agent directory" % agent_dir)
797	            return None
798	        repo = _repo_root_for(agent_dir)
799	        if not repo:
800	            res.warn("agent dir %s is not two levels below a lore-repo.md; "
801	                     "repo-scoped steps (pull, version) will be skipped" % agent_dir)
802	        fm = parse_frontmatter(read_text(role))
803	        return {
804	            "name": os.path.basename(agent_dir),
805	            "dir": agent_dir,
806	            "repo": repo,
807	            "description": fm.get("description"),
808	            "has_lore_context": os.path.isfile(
809	                os.path.join(agent_dir, "lore-context.md")),
810	        }
811	
812	    _, agents = discover_workspace(workspace)
813	    matches = [a for a in agents if a["name"] == args.agent]
814	    if not matches:
815	        res.fail("agent '%s' not found in %s" % (args.agent, workspace))
816	        res.data["available_agents"] = [
817	            {"name": a["name"], "repo": a["repo"], "description": a["description"]}
818	            for a in agents
819	        ]
820	        return None
821	    if len(matches) > 1:
822	        res.warn("agent name '%s' is ambiguous across %d repos; using %s"
823	                 % (args.agent, len(matches), matches[0]["repo"]))
824	    return matches[0]
825	
826	
827	def cmd_preflight(args, res):
828	    """The boot/attach/consult/merge preflight. This function's steps ARE the
829	    normative procedure (docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract) — if
830	    this script cannot run, read the six steps below (and the referenced
831	    helper functions for their own exact commands) and execute them by hand.
832	
833	    Step 1: resolve <framework-root> (this script's grandparent dir, or
834	    --framework-root) and read its VERSION file. Missing/unreadable VERSION is
835	    a warning, not a failure — the version check below just becomes
836	    inconclusive.
837	
838	    Step 2: resolve the target agent. If --agent-dir was given, use it
839	    directly: require a role.md there, and derive the repo root by walking up
840	    two directories and checking for lore-repo.md (warn, don't fail, if that
841	    check comes up empty — repo-scoped steps 4-5 below are skipped for a
842	    repo-less agent). Otherwise, discover every repo under <workspace> and
843	    match --agent by name; no match is a failure (report available_agents);
844	    more than one match is a warning (use the first, arbitrarily). See
845	    _resolve_agent below for the exact logic.
846	
847	    Step 3: record the agent's role.md and lore-context.md paths as
848	    `read_next`. Reading and interpreting those files is the caller's job —
849	    their content is judgment material, not something this script parses.
850	
851	    Step 4: if the agent sits under a real lore repo, auto-pull it (TTL-cached
852	    — see pull_repo below for the exact `git pull --ff-only` invocation, the
853	    fast-fail env vars, and the `.git/lr-last-pull` TTL stamp file), then
854	    compare the repo's lore-repo.md version stamp against the framework
855	    VERSION read in Step 1 (see compare_versions below for the exact match/
856	    skew rules). A failed pull is a warning ("continue in degraded mode"), not
857	    a stopping condition. Version skew other than an exact match is also a
858	    warning pointing at docs/version-check.md.
859	
860	    Step 5: otherwise (no enclosing repo — the warning from Step 2), skip both
861	    of those: report pull as "skipped" and version as "unknown", and run no
862	    git subprocess at all.
863	
864	    Step 6: detect an Agent-Teams teammate spawn (see detect_teammate below for
865	    the exact `ps -o args= -p <ppid>` walk and the `--agent-id` flag-boundary
866	    match), unless --no-teammate-check suppressed it.
867	    """
868	    workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
869	    root = resolve_framework_root(args.framework_root)
870	    fw_version = framework_version(root)
871	    if fw_version is None:
872	        res.warn("no readable VERSION at %s — version check will be inconclusive" % root)
873	
874	    res.data["framework_root"] = root
875	    res.data["workspace"] = workspace
876	
877	    agent = _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace)
878	    if agent is None:
879	        return res
880	
881	    role_file = os.path.join(agent["dir"], "role.md")
882	    lore_context_file = os.path.join(agent["dir"], "lore-context.md")
883	    if not agent["has_lore_context"]:
884	        res.warn("no lore-context.md at %s — agent has no compacted knowledge yet"
885	                 % agent["dir"])
886	
887	    res.data["agent"] = {
888	        "name": agent["name"],
889	        "dir": agent["dir"],
890	        "repo": agent["repo"],
891	        "description": agent["description"],
892	        "role_file": role_file,
893	        "lore_context_file": lore_context_file if agent["has_lore_context"] else None,
894	    }
895	    # The files the caller must read into context next. Reading them is the
896	    # caller's job: their content is judgment material, not script output.
897	    res.data["read_next"] = [p for p in (role_file, lore_context_file if
898	                                         agent["has_lore_context"] else None) if p]
899	
900	    if agent["repo"]:
901	        # Step 4 (repo branch): TTL-cached pull, then version compare.
902	        res.data["pull"] = pull_repo(
903	            agent["repo"], ttl=args.ttl, fresh=args.fresh, do_pull=not args.no_pull)
904	        if res.data["pull"]["status"] == "failed":
905	            res.warn("auto-pull failed for %s: %s — continue in degraded mode"
906	                     % (agent["repo"], res.data["pull"]["detail"]))
907	        repo_version = parse_frontmatter(
908	            read_text(os.path.join(agent["repo"], "lore-repo.md"))).get("version")
909	        res.data["version"] = compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version)
910	        if res.data["version"]["verdict"] in ("repo-behind", "repo-ahead", "differs"):
911	            res.warn("version skew: repo=%s framework=%s (%s) — see version-check.md"
912	                     % (repo_version, fw_version, res.data["version"]["verdict"]))
913	    else:
914	        # Step 5 (no-repo branch): hardcode skipped/unknown, no subprocess runs.
915	        res.data["pull"] = {"status": "skipped", "detail": "no enclosing lore repo"}
916	        res.data["version"] = {"verdict": "unknown", "repo": None,
917	                               "framework": fw_version,
918	                               "detail": "no enclosing lore repo"}
919	
920	    # Step 6: teammate detection, unless suppressed.
921	    res.data["teammate"] = detect_teammate() if not args.no_teammate_check else {
922	        "verdict": "unknown", "detail": "check suppressed by --no-teammate-check",
923	        "ancestors": []}
924	    return res
925	
926	
927	def cmd_scan(args, res):
928	    """Manual fallback: build a lore-topic manifest by hand.
929	
930	    Step 1: resolve the agent directory — either --agent-dir directly, or
931	    discover-and-match --agent by name against the workspace (see
932	    _resolve_agent below, same resolution as preflight Step 2).
933	
934	    Step 2: list <agent-dir>/lore/*.md (skip dotfiles, and skip anything that
935	    is a directory rather than a file, since a dir named `x.md` is not a
936	    topic).
937	
938	    Step 3: for each topic, take the first non-blank line, strip leading `#`
939	    markdown-heading markers and surrounding `*`/`_` emphasis, and truncate to
940	    120 chars — that's its title (see topic_title below).
941	
942	    Step 4: get each topic's last-modified date in ONE `git log
943	    --format=%x1e%cI --name-only -- <lore-dir>` call scoped to the agent's
944	    enclosing repo (see git_last_modified below) rather than one `git log`
945	    per topic. If git could not run at all, record that as `git_error` and do
946	    not claim the topics are undated — say the dates are unavailable, not that
947	    the files are new/untracked.
948	
949	    Step 5: flag a topic `stale` when its age in days exceeds --stale-days
950	    (default 180). Report topic count, stale count, and undated count
951	    alongside the per-topic list.
952	    """
953	    if args.agent_dir:
954	        agent_dir = os.path.abspath(args.agent_dir)
955	    else:
956	        workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)
957	        agent = _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace)
958	        if agent is None:
959	            return res
960	        agent_dir = agent["dir"]
961	
962	    if not os.path.isdir(agent_dir):
963	        res.fail("agent directory not found: %s" % agent_dir)
964	        return res
965	
966	    data, err = scan_lore(agent_dir, stale_days=args.stale_days)
967	    if err:
968	        res.fail(err)
969	        res.data["agent_dir"] = agent_dir
970	        return res
971	    data["agent_dir"] = agent_dir
972	    res.data = data
973	    if data.get("git_error"):
974	        # Dates are missing because git could not run, not because the topics
975	        # are new. Saying "uncommitted or untracked" here would be a confident
976	        # wrong explanation.
977	        res.warn("could not read git history, so no topic dates or staleness "
978	                 "flags are available: %s" % data["git_error"])
979	    elif data["undated_count"]:
980	        res.warn("%d topic(s) have no git date (uncommitted or untracked)"
981	                 % data["undated_count"])
982	    return res
983	
984	
985	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
986	# CLI
987	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
988	
989	def build_parser():
990	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
991	        prog="lr-core",
992	        description="Deterministic substrate for the mechanical steps of lore skills.")
993	    parser.add_argument("--framework-root", default=None,
994	                        help="Override framework root (default: this script's parent).")
995	    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command")
996	
997	    p_disc = sub.add_parser("discover", help="Enumerate lore agent repos and agents.")
998	    p_disc.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
999	    p_disc.set_defaults(func=cmd_discover)
1000	
1001	    p_pre = sub.add_parser("preflight", help="Boot/attach preflight for one agent.")
1002	    p_pre.add_argument("--agent", default=None, help="Agent name to resolve.")
1003	    p_pre.add_argument("--agent-dir", default=None,
1004	                       help="Explicit agent directory (skips discovery).")
1005	    p_pre.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
1006	    p_pre.add_argument("--ttl", type=int, default=DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC,
1007	                       help="Skip the pull if one succeeded within N seconds "
1008	                            "(default: %d; 0 disables the cache)." % DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC)
1009	    p_pre.add_argument("--fresh", action="store_true",
1010	                       help="Bypass the TTL cache and always pull.")
1011	    p_pre.add_argument("--no-pull", action="store_true", help="Skip the pull entirely.")
1012	    p_pre.add_argument("--no-teammate-check", action="store_true",
1013	                       help="Skip process-ancestry inspection.")
1014	    p_pre.set_defaults(func=cmd_preflight)
1015	
1016	    p_scan = sub.add_parser("scan", help="Manifest of an agent's lore topics.")
1017	    p_scan.add_argument("--agent", default=None, help="Agent name to resolve.")
1018	    p_scan.add_argument("--agent-dir", default=None, help="Explicit agent directory.")
1019	    p_scan.add_argument("--workspace", default=".", help="Workspace root (default: cwd).")
1020	    p_scan.add_argument("--stale-days", type=int, default=DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS,
1021	                        help="Flag topics older than N days (default: %d)."
1022	                             % DEFAULT_STALE_DAYS)
1023	    p_scan.set_defaults(func=cmd_scan)
1024	
1025	    return parser
1026	
1027	
1028	def main(argv):
1029	    parser = build_parser()
1030	    try:
1031	        args = parser.parse_args(argv)
1032	    except SystemExit as exc:
1033	        # argparse prints usage to stderr and exits. `--help` (code 0) keeps its
1034	        # normal behavior; a usage error must still honor the output contract,
1035	        # so a caller parsing stdout sees a well-formed failure rather than
1036	        # nothing at all.
1037	        if exc.code == 0:
1038	            raise
1039	        return emit_fatal("invalid invocation (run with --help for usage)")
1040	    if not getattr(args, "command", None):
1041	        parser.print_help(sys.stderr)
1042	        return emit_fatal("no subcommand given (run with --help for usage)")
1043	    if args.command in ("preflight", "scan") and not (args.agent or args.agent_dir):
1044	        return emit_fatal("%s requires --agent or --agent-dir" % args.command)
1045	
1046	    res = Result()
1047	    try:
1048	        args.func(args, res)
1049	    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - deliberate catch-all
1050	        # Any unexpected failure is exit 2: the caller falls back to the prose
1051	        # procedure rather than proceeding on partial data.
1052	        return emit_fatal("%s: %s" % (type(exc).__name__, exc))
1053	    return res.emit()
1054	
1055	
1056	if __name__ == "__main__":
1057	    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
1058	
```

461	class TestTeammateMarkerMatching(unittest.TestCase):
462	    """The marker must match as a flag, not as a substring.

500	        ]

### Script Fallback Contract

New section in `docs/conventions.md`. When a doc delegates to a script and the script fails to
complete, the engine must (1) tell the user in one line which script failed, (2) execute the
canonical prose procedure by hand to the same end state, and (3) diagnose briefly without stalling —
never abort the surrounding flow. A script that exits 0 while *reporting* a problem has succeeded;
that is data, not a takeover trigger.

The contract distinguishes two kinds of script. An **accelerator** has a canonical prose procedure
behind it, so the agent can take over by hand — `scripts/lr-core` is the reference case. An
**implementation** script *is* the specification (`scripts/workspace-pull`, `scripts/session-takeover`,
`scripts/sync-cursor-skills`, `scripts/lr-emit` + `scripts/wait-server.py`, `scripts/lrb.py`); there
is nothing to take over, so the agent reports the failure and stops that operation rather than
improvising an undocumented substitute. `scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper) is the strictest case of
that rule: it enforces budget caps and kill-switches, and a model standing in for it would be exactly

=== conventions.md Script Fallback Contract, accelerator def ===
## Script Fallback Contract

A shipped script is one of two things, and which one decides what happens when it breaks.

- **Accelerator** — a **literate** one: the procedure lives in the script's own instructional
  comments (its docstrings and inline `# Step N:` blocks), not in a separate `docs/` doc. The
  running code is normative, and so are its comments — there is exactly one artifact, not a
  prose copy and a code copy that can drift apart. A doc that delegates to it carries only a
  short pointer: what to call, what the output fields mean, and — on failure — *which function*
  to go read. `scripts/lr-core` is the reference case: `docs/agent-boot.md`,
  `docs/auto-pull.md`, `docs/attach.md`, `docs/consult.md`, `docs/lore-search.md`,
  `docs/process-merge.md`, and `docs/pull-lore.md` all point into specific functions in it
  rather than restating what those functions do.
- **Implementation** — the script *is* the specification; there is no prose procedure and no
  comment-as-procedure to execute by hand either. `scripts/workspace-pull`,
  `scripts/session-takeover`, `scripts/sync-cursor-skills`, `scripts/lr-emit` +
  `scripts/wait-server.py`, and `scripts/lrb.py` are all this kind.

**Take over manually only for a literate accelerator.** When a doc delegates a step to one and
the script **fails to complete** — a non-zero exit other than a documented result code, a missing
interpreter, no output, or output you cannot parse:

1. **Notify the user immediately**, in one line: which script failed and that you are proceeding
   manually. Never fail silently, and never let a script failure look like the underlying
   operation failing.
2. **Read the relevant function(s) in the script** — the doc's pointer names them — and **execute
   the steps described in their comments**, in order, resolving what you reasonably can along the
   way (a missing directory, a repo that needs a different path). The flow must reach the same end
   state it would have reached had the script worked.
3. **Diagnose briefly, don't stall.** If the cause is obvious (no `python3`, non-executable file,
   wrong path), say so in one line. Otherwise finish the flow and report the failure at the end for
```

Commands that silently assume GNU coreutils break there, and the failure is often invisible: a `command not found` (exit 127) inside a best-effort flow reads as the underlying operation failing, not as a portability bug.
/Users/yaroslav/Documents/agent-workspace/.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/docs/conventions.md:133:- **No GNU-only binaries.** Most notably `timeout` / `gtimeout` — absent on a stock macOS. To bound a command's runtime, use your Bash tool's own timeout parameter, not a `timeout` wrapper. (This exact trap silently disabled v13 auto-pull on macOS; see `auto-pull.md` § Step 2.) Inside a **standalone script** there is no Bash-tool timeout and no portable one-liner for a hard wall-clock cap — prevent hangs at the source with fail-fast env vars (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` with `BatchMode`/`ConnectTimeout`), or implement a bash watchdog (background the job, then `sleep`+`kill`). `scripts/workspace-pull` takes the fail-fast-env-vars route.
/Users/yaroslav/Documents/agent-workspace/.worktrees/lore-framework/lr-core-v31/docs/conventions.md:355:Both declare an explicit empty write-set: the gate parses the body, finds no globs, contributes nothing to the intersection, and proceeds without deferral. This is **not** the same as omitting the `## Write Paths` heading entirely — an absent heading triggers the conservative blanket-dirty fallback (the gate cannot tell whether the author meant "writes nothing" or forgot to declare).

=== conventions.md 160-200 (contract triggers + literate bar) ===
  `scripts/session-takeover`, `scripts/sync-cursor-skills`, `scripts/lr-emit` +
  `scripts/wait-server.py`, and `scripts/lrb.py` are all this kind.

**Take over manually only for a literate accelerator.** When a doc delegates a step to one and
the script **fails to complete** — a non-zero exit other than a documented result code, a missing
interpreter, no output, or output you cannot parse:

1. **Notify the user immediately**, in one line: which script failed and that you are proceeding
   manually. Never fail silently, and never let a script failure look like the underlying
   operation failing.
2. **Read the relevant function(s) in the script** — the doc's pointer names them — and **execute
   the steps described in their comments**, in order, resolving what you reasonably can along the
   way (a missing directory, a repo that needs a different path). The flow must reach the same end
   state it would have reached had the script worked.
3. **Diagnose briefly, don't stall.** If the cause is obvious (no `python3`, non-executable file,
   wrong path), say so in one line. Otherwise finish the flow and report the failure at the end for
   follow-up. **Never abort the surrounding flow because a script died.**

**Distinguish failure from a reported degraded condition.** A script that exits 0 while reporting a problem in its output (`"pull": {"status": "failed"}`, `"teammate": {"verdict": "unknown"}`) has *succeeded* — it did its job and told you the truth about the world. That is data: handle it per the procedure doc's degraded-mode guidance. No takeover, no user-facing script-failure notice.

Accelerator scripts state their exit-code contract in their own header. `scripts/lr-core` is the reference implementation: exit 0 = ran to completion (`ok: false` still means "ran fine, request could not be satisfied" — act on `errors`); exit 2 = could not complete, fall back.

**Never improvise a substitute for an implementation script.** There is no prose procedure to fall back to, and hand-rolling one produces a partial, undocumented imitation whose divergence nobody can review. When one of these fails: report it to the user with the exact command and the error, stop *that operation*, and continue the surrounding session. Never report the operation as done.

`scripts/lrb.py` (the Being Keeper) is the strictest case of that rule. The Keeper is substrate that must never be impersonated by a model — it enforces budget caps and kill-switches, and an LLM standing in for it would be exactly the prompt-theater the Lore Beings design forbids. Its failure mode is "Keeper down, beings do not run".

Docs that delegate to a script carry **one line** pointing here, whichever kind it is — a pointer, not a restatement, so this contract has a single home.

**The literate-accelerator pattern, generalized.** Writing an accelerator's procedure as
instructions-shaped comments *inside* the script — rather than as prose in a companion doc — closes
the gap a plain "accelerator" leaves open: if the fallback lives in a separate file, nothing stops
it from silently drifting out of sync with what the code actually does the next time either one is
edited alone. Collapsing procedure and implementation into one artifact means the fallback is
exactly as current as the code that just failed, by construction. The bar for a comment to count:
it must read as a freestanding instruction a reader could execute *without running the code*
("run `git -C <repo> pull --ff-only` with these two env vars"), not as an annotation of what the
adjacent line does ("call git pull"). Reach for this pattern for any future accelerator script;
see `literate-accelerator-pattern.md` in the lore-architect's lore for the fuller writeup and the
first instance (`scripts/lr-core`, v31).

## Migration / Release-Note Authoring
```

#### Result

```text
1	# Auto-Pull (Single-Repo Refresh)
2	
3	> **Audience note.** This is an internal procedure doc — Claude reads it from `agent-boot.md`, `attach.md`, `process-merge.md`, and `pull-lore.md`. There is no `/lr:auto-pull` skill. Users invoke its behavior implicitly via boot/attach/merge or explicitly via `/lr:pull-lore`.
4	
5	The shared procedure for refreshing one lore agent repo's git state mid-flow. Used by:
6	
7	- **`agent-boot.md` Step 1** — auto-pull the host repo at boot time (via `lr-core preflight`).
8	- **`attach.md`** — auto-pull the guest repo before reading its role/lore-context.
9	- **`process-merge.md` step 0** — defense-in-depth pull right before the merge subagent reads its lore.
10	- **`docs/pull-lore.md`** — the user-invoked `/lr:pull-lore` skill iterates this procedure across active agents.
11	
12	The procedure is intentionally narrow: one `git pull --ff-only` against the agent's repo, with safety gates and a degraded-mode failure path. No clone logic (that's `/lr:workspace-pull`), no migration logic (that's `version-check.md`), no commit or push.
13	
14	## Normative source
15	
16	**The procedure lives in `pull_repo()`'s own comments in `scripts/lr-core`** (a literate
17	accelerator, `docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract) — this doc is a pointer into it,
18	not a second copy. Boot/attach/consult/merge reach it through `lr-core preflight`, which also adds
19	a TTL cache on top (a pull that succeeded within the window is reported `fresh` and skipped — see
20	`_read_stamp`/`_write_stamp` next to `pull_repo`).
21	
22	**When the script cannot run:** notify the user, then read `pull_repo()`'s docstring in
23	`scripts/lr-core` and execute its five steps by hand against `<lore-agent-repo>` — the exact `git`
24	invocations, the fail-fast env vars, the bare-repo and no-origin skip cases, and the
25	git-could-not-run-at-all-is-a-failure-not-a-skip distinction are all spelled out there. One thing
26	stays here because it's caller-side policy the script doesn't decide, not implementation detail it
27	duplicates — how verbose to be about the outcome, below.
28	
29	## Inputs
30	
31	- `<lore-agent-repo>` — absolute path to the lore agent repo to refresh.
32	
33	## Report
34	
35	The verbosity rule depends on the calling site. Boot/attach/merge are quiet on the no-op case (the common path) so the surrounding flow stays uncluttered; `/lr:pull-lore` is always verbose because it's user-invoked.
36	
37	| Outcome | Boot / attach / merge | `/lr:pull-lore` |
38	|---|---|---|
39	| Already up to date | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: already up to date` |
40	| Fast-forwarded | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pulled <N> commit(s)` |
41	| Skipped (not a git repo / bare repo / no origin) | silent | print `<lore-agent-repo>: skipped — <reason>` |
42	| Failed (non-FF, network, auth, **git could not run**) | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` | print `<lore-agent-repo>: pull failed — <error>` |
43	
44	For the commit count, `git rev-list HEAD@{1}..HEAD --count` works after a fast-forward; if it fails or returns 0 unexpectedly, just print `pulled` without a count.
45	
46	Never abort the surrounding flow on a pull failure. The agent must always finish booting / attaching / merging even if its repo is momentarily out of sync.
47	
48	## Worktrees
49	
50	The lore framework's worktree convention (see `worktrees.md`) keeps top-level repo checkouts on their default branch and pushes feature work into `<workspace>/.worktrees/<repo>/<slug>/`. Auto-pull operates on whichever path was passed in — the path discovered by boot/attach.
51	
52	- **Booted from a top-level checkout:** auto-pull fast-forwards the default branch as expected. This is the common path.
53	- **Booted from inside a worktree** (the discovered path resolves under `.worktrees/`): auto-pull runs against that worktree's checkout. If the worktree is on a feature branch with no upstream, or the upstream has diverged, the pull fails with a clear error and the surrounding flow continues in degraded mode. Manage feature-branch updates manually — auto-pull is best-effort.
54	
55	`/lr:pull-lore` and the boot/attach/merge sites all delegate to this same procedure, so the worktree behavior is uniform.
56	
57	## Invariants
58	
59	- **Best-effort.** A pull failure never blocks boot, attach, merge, or any user-visible flow. The surrounding flow continues in degraded mode with a visible warning.
60	- **No destructive actions.** Never `--force`, never `git reset`, never `git checkout` away from the user's branch. If a fast-forward isn't possible, the failure surfaces and the user resolves manually.
61	- **Uncommitted changes are preserved.** `--ff-only` either advances `HEAD` cleanly past the dirty tree or refuses with a clear error — both outcomes are safe. Auto-pull never stashes, resets, or otherwise touches the working tree.
62	- **Per-repo scope.** Auto-pull operates on a single repo at a time. Multi-repo flows (e.g., `/lr:pull-lore` over host + guests) iterate this procedure per repo.
63	- **Idempotent.** Running auto-pull twice in a row on a clean repo produces no observable difference past the first run.
64	
65	## Distinct From
66	
67	- **`/lr:workspace-pull`** — filesystem-wide: discovers `lore-repo.md` files, clones missing repos declared in `repos:`, pulls every top-level git repo. Auto-pull is single-repo and never clones.
68	- **`version-check.md`** — repo-version reconciliation: applies migrations and stamps `lore-repo.md` after a framework `VERSION` bump. Auto-pull is git-only and never modifies file contents.
69	- **`resolve-conflicts.md`** — finalize-time merge of remote conflicts in agent subtrees. Triggered only when push is rejected; auto-pull is the *prevention* arm of the same problem.
70	
71	The three sit on a spectrum: auto-pull keeps the local repo fresh (so the rest of the flow reads current data); `/lr:workspace-pull` keeps the workspace fresh (so all repos are present and up to date); resolve-conflicts heals after a concurrent finalize collided. They compose without overlap.
72	
73	## See Also
74	
75	- `pull-lore.md` — the `/lr:pull-lore` skill that user-invokes this procedure mid-session.
76	- `agent-boot.md` — boot-time invocation site.
77	- `attach.md` — guest-attach invocation site.
78	- `process-merge.md` — pre-merge invocation site (defense-in-depth atop boot pull).
79	- `workspace-pull.md` — workspace-wide companion.
80	- `conventions.md` § Tooling: CWD Safety — why `git -C <repo>` instead of `cd`.
81	
```

=== pull-lore.md:16 ===

1. **Enumerates active agents** — host (whichever agent was booted) plus any guests attached via `/lr:attach`.
2. **Auto-pulls each agent's repo** — runs the procedure in `<framework-root>/docs/auto-pull.md` per repo. When two active agents share a repo, that repo is pulled once.
3. **Re-reads each active agent's `role.md` and `lore-context.md`** — so any changes pulled in actually take effect in working memory. Without this step, the pulled files are on disk but the host is still operating from the pre-pull context.
4. **Reports a one-line summary per repo** — pulled / already up to date / failed / skipped (no origin), so the user can see what changed.

It is **not** loaded for normal `/lr:boot` sessions or for the team lead. 117	- `docs/being.md`, `docs/takeover.md`, `docs/wait.md`, `docs/workspace-pull.md` — one-line Script
118	  Fallback Contract pointers at their script-delegation sites (unaffected by the literate-
119	  accelerator change — these are all Implementation scripts).
120	- `docs/spawn-teammate.md`, `docs/teammate-conventions.md` — teammate-rule loading now cites
121	  `agent-boot.md` Step 2 (was Step 5 before the boot doc was renumbered).
122	- `docs/conventions.md` — § Script Fallback Contract's Accelerator category redefined as
123	  **literate**: the procedure lives in the script's own comments, not a companion doc (see below);
124	  § Tooling: Non-Shell Runtimes gains the deterministic-substrate class alongside the protocol-
125	  server one.
126	
127	### Literate Accelerator
128	
129	An accelerator script's procedure now lives in its own instructional comments — docstrings and
130	inline `# Step N:` blocks written so a reader could execute each step without running the code —
131	rather than in a separate prose doc. This removes the last drift risk in the accelerator model:
132	previously the same procedure existed twice (once as code, once as doc prose), and the two could
133	diverge from each other. `scripts/lr-core` is the first and, so far, only instance. See
134	`docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract for the rule, and the lore-architect's
135	`literate-accelerator-pattern.md` for the fuller writeup.
136	
137	## Compatibility
138	
139	No behavior change for agents or repos. Every flow reaches the same end state as in v30; only the
140	number of tool calls it takes to get there is different. An install whose `python3` is missing or
141	whose cache is stale keeps working through the fallback path.
142

```text
1	# Literate Accelerator Pattern
2	
3	A refinement of the **Accelerator** half of the Script Fallback Contract
4	(`lore-framework/docs/conventions.md` § Script Fallback Contract): instead of a script that
5	accelerates a procedure documented separately in a `docs/*.md` file, the procedure lives inside the
6	script itself, as instructional comments — docstrings and inline `# Step N:` blocks written as
7	freestanding instructions a reader could execute *without running the code*. The script is one
8	artifact that serves two purposes: it runs, and its own text is the fallback spec when it can't.
9	
10	## The problem this solves
11	
12	Plain "Accelerator" (prose doc normative, script implements it) still leaves two versions of the
13	same truth in two files. Nothing stops them from drifting: someone tweaks the script's logic and
14	forgets the doc, or edits the doc's wording and the script quietly no longer matches it. Worse,
15	the doc is the thing a caller falls back to exactly when the script — the thing most likely to
16	have been kept current by whoever last touched the *behavior* — has just failed. The fallback the
17	framework trusts most, in the moment it's needed most, is the one most likely to have rotted.
18	
19	Collapsing procedure and implementation into one file removes the seam. There's nothing left to
20	drift apart, because there's only one thing.
21	
22	## The one rule that makes it work
23	
24	**A comment must read as an instruction, not as an annotation of the adjacent code.**
25	
26	- Not this: `# call git pull` (describes what the next line does — useless if you can't run it)
27	- This: `` Run `git -C <repo> pull --ff-only` with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and
28	  `GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10'` so an unauthenticated remote fails
29	  fast instead of hanging on a prompt. `` (a step a person could execute by hand, with the exact
30	  command and the reason for each flag)
31	
32	Concretely this means: exact commands with exact flags, exact file paths (not "the stamp file" but
33	`<absolute-git-dir>/lr-last-pull`), and the decision rules for branching (what counts as "fresh,"
34	what "skip" vs "fail" means and why the distinction matters). If a step references another
35	function for its detail, name that function so the reader knows where to look next — don't
36	inline a partial restatement.
37	
38	## What changes in the doc/script relationship
39	
40	- The script's module docstring and each `cmd_*`/helper function's docstring **are** the
41	  procedure. A doc that delegates to it carries a **short pointer**: what to call, what the output
42	  fields mean, and — on failure — *which function* names the steps to execute.
43	  `docs/agent-boot.md`, `docs/auto-pull.md`, `docs/attach.md`, `docs/consult.md`,
44	  `docs/lore-search.md`, `docs/process-merge.md`, and `docs/pull-lore.md` all follow this now for
45	  `scripts/lr-core`.
46	- Conceptual framing that *isn't* duplicated by the script stays in the doc — e.g. `auto-pull.md`
47	  keeps the per-calling-site reporting-verbosity table (boot/attach/merge silent vs. `/lr:pull-lore`
48	  verbose), because that's caller-side policy the script doesn't decide, not a restatement of what
49	  `pull_repo()` does.
50	- On a script failure, the caller reads the named function's comments in the script itself and
51	  executes those steps by hand — not a separate prose doc.
52	
53	## First instance
54	
55	`scripts/lr-core`, v31 (`wip/lr-core-v31`, parked — see `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`). The
56	redesign was applied across the whole script: the module docstring's "WHAT THIS IS NOT" claim
57	flipped from "procedure docs remain normative" to naming the script's own comments as the spec;
58	`cmd_preflight`/`cmd_discover`/`cmd_scan` each gained numbered step docstrings; `pull_repo`,
59	`compare_versions`, `detect_teammate`, and `_resolve_agent` had their docstrings tightened from
60	"why we did this" framing to "do this — here's why," with exact commands/paths/rules spelled out.
61	No behavioral change — comments and surrounding prose only.
62	
63	## Applying this to future scripts
64	
65	Reach for this pattern whenever a new accelerator script is proposed: write its procedure as the
66	script's own instructional comments from the start, rather than drafting a companion prose doc and
67	mechanizing it later. The distinction from **Implementation** scripts
68	(`scripts/workspace-pull`, `scripts/lrb.py`, etc. — the script *is* the spec, nothing to fall back
69	to) is unchanged; this pattern only applies to the Accelerator half of the split.
70	
71	See `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md` for a related but distinct classification
72	question (what a *subagent* is for, not what a *script's* fallback is) and
73	`single-canonical-source-discipline.md` for the general principle this is one instance of.
74	
```

  "new_string": "Collapsing procedure and implementation into one file removes the seam. There's nothing left to\ndrift apart, because there's only one thing.\n\n## The cost: the pattern cannot self-serve when the artifact is gone\n\nThe seam this removes was also a redundancy. A plain accelerator's fallback lived in a *different*\nfile from the thing that broke, so it was present by construction; a literate accelerator's\nfallback rides in the artifact that just failed. That is fine for the common failures — a bad exit,\na missing interpreter, unparsable output all leave the file readable — but it has no floor when the\nscript is **missing, truncated, or unreadable**. The pattern then specifies nothing at all, and an\nexecutor left to improvise from a function name produces a silent partial imitation of the\nprocedure, which is worse than a reported inability to run it.\n\nSo any literate accelerator must **name its floor explicitly** in the contract rather than leaving\nthe executor to discover the gap mid-failure: recover the procedure from git history or another\ninstall, and failing that, say plainly which operation cannot be performed. This was found by the\nhand-executor lens in v31's trilens round 1 — the doc set covered every failure mode *except* the\none that makes the design different from its predecessor. Generalizes: when a design removes a\nredundancy, audit the case the redundancy used to cover."
}

```text
1	# Finalization Process
2	
3	Run the full session finalization: reflect → merge → summarize → commit and push. Triggered by `/lr:finalize` at the end of a session.
4	
5	This doc orchestrates the four phases. Phases 1–3 are defined in their own process docs — read them as each phase begins. Phase 4 (commit and push) is defined here, since it's orchestration-level logic rather than a reusable subroutine.
6	
7	## Relationship to the individual skills
8	
9	Finalize composes three existing skills plus a final commit step:
10	
11	- `/lr:reflect` alone — phase 1 only, no finalize commit
12	- `/lr:merge` alone — phase 2 only, no finalize commit
13	- `/lr:summarize` alone — phase 3 only, no finalize commit
14	- `/lr:finalize` — all three, then commit and push in phase 4
15	
16	When a phase is invoked standalone, the user is responsible for committing whatever they want to keep. Finalize is the only path that commits and pushes automatically.
17	
18	## Phase 1 — Reflect
19	
20	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and follow it. Writes reflection topics into each active agent's `reflections/` directory. Reflect runs **inline**, host-first, per active agent — it needs session context (which a fresh-booted subagent wouldn't have), so the iteration stays in the host session. This is intentionally different from phase 2.
21	
22	## Phase 2 — Merge
23	
24	Read `<framework-root>/docs/process-merge.md` and follow it. Merge runs in a **subagent per active agent, in parallel**; each subagent boots as its target agent and then integrates that agent's reflections into its `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, and `role.md`. Cleans up `reflections/`. **Does not commit** — phase 4 covers it.
25	
26	Merge is parallelizable precisely because it is file-driven (`reflections/` + `lore/`) and doesn't need session context — the contrast with phase 1.
27	
28	**Preserve each merge subagent's return value through to phase 3.** Summarize composes each guest summary from (a) the host summary, (b) session memory, and (c) the merge subagent's return for that guest. If the returns aren't retained, phase 3 loses the list of lore changes per guest.
29	
30	## Phase 3 — Summarize
31	
32	Read `<framework-root>/docs/summarize.md` and follow it. Writes the canonical summary into the host agent's `sessions/YYYY/MM/` directory, the host full Markdown session log into `archive/YYYY/MM/`, and — for every attached guest that had lore updates in phase 2 — a short guest summary into the guest's `sessions/YYYY/MM/`. All summaries and the full log share the session UUID. Summarize is additive — its failure does not roll back reflect or merge.
33	
34	Phase 3 includes summarize Step 1.5: resolving the native session log and writing the host full Markdown archive plus `usage`/`archive` frontmatter. This is a **required attempt before phase 4**, not an optional enhancement. Skip it only when Step 1.5's command-level rules say to skip (no resolved log path, non-zero stats/archive command, privacy concern, or another explicit error). Before committing, verify the host summary either contains both `usage:` and `archive:` and the archive Markdown file exists with matching frontmatter, or that you have reported the exact Step 1.5 reason for omitting them.
35	
36	## Phase 4 — Commit and Push
37	
38	Collect every repo touched by phases 1–3 — each active agent's repo (for its own lore updates), plus the host's repo (for the canonical summary and archive), plus any guest repo that received a short guest summary from phase 3. When host and guests share a repo, their changes go into a single commit for that repo. Then, for each such repo:
39	
40	1. `git -C <repo> add agents/` — scoped to the agent tree so incidental untracked files elsewhere are not swept in.
41	2. `git -C <repo> commit -m "Finalize session <short-uuid>"`.
42	3. `git -C <repo> push`.
43	
44	For each repo, print a one-line confirmation (e.g., `✓ <repo>: committed <sha>, pushed to <branch>`). No approval prompt — phase 4 runs end-to-end without user interaction.
45	
46	### Failure handling
47	
48	- **Summarize failed** — commit the reflect+merge output alone. Merge output is valuable on its own; don't hold it hostage to the summary.
49	- **Any merge subagent failed** — do not commit in that repo. Report the failure and let the user resolve before retrying finalize.
50	- **Push rejected due to conflicts in an agent subtree** — this happens when another user (or parallel session) finalized the same agent concurrently. Trigger the conflict-resolution procedure: read `<framework-root>/docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and follow it. One subagent is spawned per conflicted agent; each boots as its agent, reconciles its own subtree, and retries push up to 3 times against concurrent races.
51	- **Push fails for other reasons** (auth, remote unreachable, conflicts outside agent subtrees) — the commit is already made locally. Report the failure and let the user resolve manually.
52	
53	### Cross-repo guests
54	
55	If a guest is attached from a different lore agent repo, phase 2 touches that guest's repo and phase 4 commits there too. The guest also receives a short guest summary in its own repo (see `summarize.md`). Each repo gets its own commit + push.
56	
57	### Partial push failure across repos
58	
59	Push order across repos is undefined. If one repo's push succeeds and another's fails (network, auth, conflicts), the public record is momentarily asymmetric — e.g., the host summary is visible while a guest summary isn't yet. The local commits are already made; the user can retry the failed repo's push manually. Do not roll back the successful repos to "restore symmetry" — that would discard work.
60	
61	## Invariants
62	
63	- **One commit per touched repo.** Reflect, merge, and summarize output land in a single commit per repo — not split across phases.
64	- **Fully automated.** Finalize runs end-to-end without approval prompts. Phase 3's summary display in `summarize.md` step 12 is the user's view of what was recorded; git history is the post-hoc review channel.
65	- **Push is part of finalize.** Standalone reflect/merge/summarize do not push; only `/lr:finalize` does.
66	- **No empty commits.** If nothing was produced by phases 1–3 in a given repo, skip committing in that repo.
67	
68	## When to use
69	
70	Run at the end of a working session. Do not run finalize for a consultation (`/lr:consult` is already ephemeral) or for a session that produced no lore changes and no summary-worthy narrative.
71	
```
